It's just really messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself struggling to focus on a single task for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Like, when I'm in class or, um, watching a video for my business assignments, I can start out listening okay. Then I'll snap back and realize I completely missed half of what was actually said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think so. Like, when I'm in class or, um, watching a video for my business assignments, I can start out listening okay. But after just a few minutes, my mind just wanders off to new charm designs or ideas for shop orders. Then I'll snap back and realize I completely missed half of what was actually said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed that being a problem for me. I mean, my mind gets distracted a lot, obviously, but when it comes to actually doing things or making decisions... I usually think about it first. Um, especially when I have to sit through lectures or study group meetings. My leg is pretty much always bouncing. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just... messing with the beads or key rings I keep in my bag. I usually start doing it before I even really notice I am. It's just hard for me to sit completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. Um, if a teacher or maybe someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part fine. But then later on, I'll find out I completely skipped something important. I just kind of miss those details and move on before the whole thing is actually done.\n\nDoctor: Were you more restless or fidgety when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely was. Um, my mom actually still has some of my old notes from my elementary school teachers. They pretty much all say the same thing—that I was always getting out of my seat, or talking before I was actually called on. And they mentioned I was always rushing through group activities, even when I completely understood the work. So, yeah, I was definitely pretty restless back then.\n\nDoctor: Were you often described as being overly active or having too much energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that kind of goes back to what I just mentioned. Um, like with those old elementary school notes my mom kept... they basically point to me having way too much energy. I just kind of miss those details and move on before the whole thing is actually done.\n\nDoctor: Were you more restless or fidgety when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely was. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because I feel like I just can't get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. It's really frustrating. My shop supplies, school due dates, receipts, assignment notes... they all just end up in these huge piles everywhere. I look at it all and I honestly just don't know what to do first.\n\nDoctor: How much is this disorganization affecting your daily life and overall well-being?\n\nPatient: It's affecting things a lot, honestly. Because of all this, um, I actually missed some required steps for my school program recently. So now my financial aid is at risk, which is really bad. And on the shop side... the people I work with on projects have started depending on me less. They just don't give me as much to do anymore because they know I'm struggling to keep it all together. It's just really messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself struggling to focus on a single task for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Like, when I'm in class or, um, watching a video for my business assignments, I can start out listening okay. But after just a few minutes, my mind just wanders off to new charm designs or ideas for shop orders. Then I'll snap back and realize I completely missed half of what was actually said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed that being a problem for me. I mean, my mind gets distracted a lot, obviously, but when it comes to actually doing things or making decisions... I usually think about it first. I don't really just jump into stuff blindly without thinking it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Um, especially when I have to sit through lectures or study group meetings. My leg is pretty much always bouncing. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just... messing with the beads or key rings I keep in my bag. I usually start doing it before I even really notice I am. It's just hard for me to sit completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. I mean, I get stressed sometimes if I'm behind on an assignment or something normal like that, but I don't really sit around feeling worried. My brain is definitely always going, but it's usually just bouncing between random ideas, not really stressing over things.\n\nDoctor: Do you have intense emotional reactions to things that happen to you?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I react to things like anyone else would, I guess, but I wouldn't call it intense. If something bad happens I get upset, or if something good happens I get happy, but I don't really have huge blow-ups or anything like that. My emotions are usually pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or completing tasks due to not paying attention to details?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, if a teacher or maybe someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part fine. But then later on, I'll find out I completely skipped something important. Like, I might forget to actually attach a form, or I just won't finish the last part of an order note. I just kind of miss those details and move on before the whole thing is actually done.\n\nDoctor: Were you more restless or fidgety when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely was. Um, my mom actually still has some of my old notes from my elementary school teachers. They pretty much all say the same thing—that I was always getting out of my seat, or talking before I was actually called on. And they mentioned I was always rushing through group activities, even when I completely understood the work. So, yeah, I was definitely pretty restless back then.\n\nDoctor: Were you often described as being overly active or having too much energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that kind of goes back to what I just mentioned. Um, like with those old elementary school notes my mom kept... they basically point to me having way too much energy. So, yeah, I was definitely described that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really say so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes like anyone else, but I don't think I have trouble managing how I feel. My emotions seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following directions or completing tasks at work or in other areas of your life outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, like, if a teacher or someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part just fine. But then later I'll find out I completely skipped something important—like forgetting to attach a form, or not finishing the last part of an order note. So, yeah, following through on all the directions can be pretty tough for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily routine or personal space outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my room at home can get a little messy sometimes, just like normal stuff, but I don't think I have a big problem organizing my daily routine or keeping my personal space together. It's just kind of average, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you diagnosed with ADHD as a child?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't officially diagnosed when I was a kid. But, um, my mom actually still has a bunch of my old elementary school report cards and notes from teachers. They always said the same things—that I was constantly getting out of my seat, talking before it was my turn, and just kind of rushing through group activities, even when I knew how to do the work. So the signs were definitely there, I just never got a diagnosis for it back then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I ever get that locked in on one thing. If anything, I'm usually bouncing between a few different things at once. So, no, I wouldn't say I get super focused like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So, yeah, I was definitely pretty restless back then.\n\nDoctor: Were you often described as being overly active or having too much energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that kind of goes back to what I just mentioned. Um, like with those old elementary school notes my mom kept... they basically point to me having way too much energy. The teachers were always writing down that I was out of my seat, or talking before I was called on. And just rushing through group activities, even when I already understood the work. So, yeah, I was definitely described that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really say so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes like anyone else, but I don't think I have trouble managing how I feel. My emotions seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following directions or completing tasks at work or in other areas of your life outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, like, if a teacher or someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part just fine. But then later I'll find out I completely skipped something important—like forgetting to attach a form, or not finishing the last part of an order note. So, yeah, following through on all the directions can be pretty tough for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily routine or personal space outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my room at home can get a little messy sometimes, just like normal stuff, but I don't think I have a big problem organizing my daily routine or keeping my personal space together. It's just kind of average, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you diagnosed with ADHD as a child?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't officially diagnosed when I was a kid. But, um, my mom actually still has a bunch of my old elementary school report cards and notes from teachers. They always said the same things—that I was constantly getting out of my seat, talking before it was my turn, and just kind of rushing through group activities, even when I knew how to do the work. So the signs were definitely there, I just never got a diagnosis for it back then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I ever get that locked in on one thing. If anything, I'm usually bouncing between a few different things at once. So, no, I wouldn't say I get super focused like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so absorbed in an activity that you lose track of time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much like what I just mentioned—I don't really get that absorbed in anything. I'm usually switching what I'm doing or moving around too much to just sit there and lose track of time on one specific activity.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just hard for me to sit completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. My emotions are usually pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or completing tasks due to not paying attention to details?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, if a teacher or maybe someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part fine. But then later on, I'll find out I completely skipped something important. I just kind of miss those details and move on before the whole thing is actually done.\n\nDoctor: Were you more restless or fidgety when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely was. Um, my mom actually still has some of my old notes from my elementary school teachers. And they mentioned I was always rushing through group activities, even when I completely understood the work. So, yeah, I was definitely pretty restless back then.\n\nDoctor: Were you often described as being overly active or having too much energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that kind of goes back to what I just mentioned. Um, like, if a teacher or someone from a vendor meeting gives me a few steps to do, I usually handle the first part just fine. But then later I'll find out I completely skipped something important—like forgetting to attach a form, or not finishing the last part of an order note. So, yeah, following through on all the directions can be pretty tough for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily routine or personal space outside of school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's just kind of average, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you diagnosed with ADHD as a child?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't officially diagnosed when I was a kid. But, um, my mom actually still has a bunch of my old elementary school report cards and notes from teachers. So the signs were definitely there, I just never got a diagnosis for it back then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really.
